The **2026 United States House of Representatives elections in Texas** will be held on November 3, 2026, to elect the thirty-eight [U.S. representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._representative) from the [state](18618239.md) of [Texas](29810.md), one from each of the state's thirty-eight [congressional districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_congressional_districts). The elections will coincide with [other elections](78311563.md) to the House of Representatives, [elections](65785708.md) to the [United States Senate](24909346.md), and various [state and local elections](70357296.md). The primary elections took place on March 3, 2026, and in races where no candidate receives over 50% in a primary, runoff elections took place on May 26.[^cite_note-1]

## Redistricting

On July 9, 2025, the [Texas state government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Texas) announced that during a planned special session on July 21 of that year, it would tackle [mid-decade redistricting](80621571.md). This had been pushed privately by the [White House](33057.md) to help Republicans keep control of the House in 2026, and critics have labeled it a [gerrymander](12987.md).[^cite_note-2][^cite_note-3]

On August 20, 2025, the [Texas House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_House_of_Representatives) passed congressional maps that would target five Democratic-held seats. The vote was 88–52, a party-line vote. The new map changes the territory of Democratic representatives [Marc Veasey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc_Veasey), [Vicente Gonzalez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vicente_Gonzalez_\(American_politician\)), [Lloyd Doggett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Doggett), [Julie Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julie_Johnson_\(politician\)), and [Al Green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Green_\(politician\)).[^cite_note-4] On August 23, 2025, the Texas Senate passed the map with a vote 18–8. Governor [Greg Abbott](1492735.md) has signed the map into law, and therefore will be the active map used in the 2026 House elections in Texas.

On November 18, 2025, a federal court blocked Texas from using its newly drawn congressional map in the 2026 midterms, ruling that the map is likely an unconstitutional "racial gerrymander".[^cite_note-\:3-5] Three days later on November 21, [Supreme Court Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Associate_Justice_of_the_Supreme_Court_of_the_United_States) [Samuel Alito](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samuel_Alito) granted the request by the state to pause the court's ruling that reverts the election back to using the maps drawn in 2021 until the full [Supreme Court of the United States](31737.md) could make a decision.[^cite_note-rulingpause-6][^cite_note-cbsrulingpause-7] On December 4, the Supreme Court ruled that Texas can use the new map in the 2026 midterm elections, striking down the lower court's ruling.[^cite_note-8]



Map of Texas's congressional districts, as passed by the [governor of Texas](214883.md) on August 29, 2025\

## District 1

See also: [Texas's 1st congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_1st_congressional_district)



Texas's 1st congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 1st district encompasses much of [East Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Texas), including [Tyler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tyler,_Texas), [Longview](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Longview,_Texas), [Nacogdoches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nacogdoches,_Texas) and [Texarkana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texarkana,_Texas). The incumbent is Republican [Nathaniel Moran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nathaniel_Moran), who was re-elected unopposed in 2024. Donald Trump and Ted Cruz won the district in 2024 with 74.3% and 72.5% of the vote, respectively, in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28]

## District 2

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Steve Toth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Toth) Shaun Finnie   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Dan Crenshaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Crenshaw)  [Republican](32070.md) |

See also: [Texas's 2nd congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_2nd_congressional_district)



Texas's 2nd congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 2nd district encompasses most of the northern and northeastern suburbs of Houston, including [The Woodlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Woodlands,_Texas), [Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spring,_Texas), [Kingwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingwood,_Texas), [New Caney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Caney,_Texas), [Humble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humble,_Texas), and [Atascocita](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atascocita,_Texas), as well as the [Willowbrook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willowbrook,_Houston) area of Houston itself. The incumbent is Republican [Dan Crenshaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dan_Crenshaw), who was re-elected with 65.7% of the vote in 2024, will not be on the general election ballot since he was defeated in the primary by State Representative [Steve Toth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Toth).[^cite_note-HPVT-28] Donald Trump and Ted Cruz won 60.8% and 58.0% of the vote, respectively, in this district in 2024.

### Republican primary

#### Nominee

- [Steve Toth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Toth), [state representative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_House_of_Representatives) from the [15th district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_15th_House_of_Representatives_district) (2013–2015, 2019–present) and candidate for the 8th Congressional district in [2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_United_States_House_of_Representatives_elections_in_Texas#District_8)

  [^cite_note-Toth-50]

#### Eliminated in primary

  [^cite_note-51]

- Nicholas Lee Plumb, retail manager

  [^cite_note-Filing-29]

#### Withdrawn

- Jon Bonck, mortgage broker *(running in the 38th district)*

  [^cite_note-third-52]

- [Valentina Gomez](80133308.md), financial strategist and candidate for [Missouri secretary of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missouri_Secretary_of_State) in [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Missouri_Secretary_of_State_election) *(ran in the 31st district)*

  [^cite_note-VG2-53]

  [^cite_note-Filing-29]

## District 3

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Keith Self](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keith_Self) Evan Hunt   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Keith Self](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keith_Self)  [Republican](32070.md) |

See also: [Texas's 3rd congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_3rd_congressional_district)



Texas's 3rd congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 3rd district encompasses much of [Collin County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collin_County,_Texas) and [Hunt County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunt_County,_Texas) in the [Dallas–Fort Worth Metroplex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas%E2%80%93Fort_Worth_metroplex), including eastern [Plano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plano,_Texas), [McKinney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McKinney,_Texas), [Allen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allen,_Texas), [Wylie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wylie,_Texas) and [Greenville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenville,_Texas), as well as much of the [I-30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_30_in_Texas) corridor to the east including [Sulphur Springs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulphur_Springs,_Texas) and [Mount Pleasant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Pleasant,_Texas). The incumbent is Republican [Keith Self](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Keith_Self), who was re-elected with 62.5% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] Donald Trump and Ted Cruz each respectively won 60.3% and 57.7% of the vote here in 2024.

## District 4

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Pat Fallon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pat_Fallon) Jason Pearce   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |

Texas's 4th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 4th district encompasses most of the counties in the eastern part of the [Texoma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texoma) region along the [Red River](194242.md), including the communities of [Sherman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sherman,_Texas) and [Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris,_Texas), as well as some sections of the suburban and exurban [DFW Metroplex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas%E2%80%93Fort_Worth_metroplex) including [Frisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frisco,_Texas), most of [Plano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plano,_Texas) and the Collin County portion of [Dallas](53838.md). The incumbent is Republican [Pat Fallon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pat_Fallon), who was re-elected with 68.4% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] Donald Trump and Ted Cruz each respectively won 61.2% and 59.0% of the vote here in 2024.

## District 5

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Lance Gooden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lance_Gooden) Chelsey Hockett   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Lance Gooden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lance_Gooden)  [Republican](32070.md) |

See also: [Texas's 5th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_5th_congressional_district)



Texas's 5th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 5th district encompasses the southeastern parts of the [Dallas–Fort Worth Metroplex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas%E2%80%93Fort_Worth_metroplex), including [Mesquite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesquite,_Texas), [Terrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terrell,_Texas), [Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine,_Texas), [Athens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Athens,_Texas), [Canton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canton,_Texas), [Kaufman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaufman,_Texas), the southern halves of [Garland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garland,_Texas) and [Rowlett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rowlett,_Texas), and the [Lakewood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lakewood,_Dallas) and [Lake Highlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Highlands) portions of [Dallas](53838.md). The incumbent is Republican [Lance Gooden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lance_Gooden), who was re-elected with 64.1% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] Donald Trump and Ted Cruz each respectively won 60.1% and 56.9% of the vote in this district in 2024.

## District 6

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Jake Ellzey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jake_Ellzey) Danny Minton   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Jake Ellzey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jake_Ellzey)  [Republican](32070.md) |

See also: [Texas's 6th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_6th_congressional_district)



Texas's 6th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 6th district encompasses most of the southern parts of the [Dallas–Fort Worth Metroplex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas%E2%80%93Fort_Worth_metroplex), including most or all of the suburbs of [Midlothian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midlothian,_Texas), [Mansfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mansfield,_Texas), [Burleson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burleson,_Texas), [Waxahachie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waxahachie,_Texas) and [Corsicana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corsicana,_Texas), as well as most of the west side of [Arlington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington,_Texas) and south and central [Irving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irving,_Texas). The incumbent is Republican [Jake Ellzey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jake_Ellzey), who was re-elected with 66.4% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] Donald Trump and Ted Cruz won 60.4% and 57.4%, respectively, in this district in 2024.

## District 7

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Lizzie Fletcher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lizzie_Fletcher) Alexander Hale   Party [Democratic](5043544.md) [Republican](32070.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Lizzie Fletcher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lizzie_Fletcher)  [Democratic](5043544.md) |

See also: [Texas's 7th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_7th_congressional_district)



Texas's 7th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 7th district encompasses a diverse southwestern stretch of the [Greater Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Houston) area across Harris and Fort Bend counties, including [the Galleria area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uptown_Houston) of Houston and the inner West Loop neighborhoods of [Montrose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montrose,_Houston), [Meyerland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meyerland,_Houston), [Rice Military](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rice_Military,_Houston), [the Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houston_Heights) and [Greenway Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenway_Plaza), along with such diverse southwest Houston neighborhoods as [Westchase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westchase,_Houston), [Sharpstown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sharpstown,_Houston), [Gulfton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulfton,_Houston) and [Alief](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alief,_Houston), and largely Asian and Hispanic portions of western Sugar Land and Mission Bend in Fort Bend County. The incumbent is Democrat [Lizzie Fletcher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lizzie_Fletcher), who was re-elected with 61.2% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] The diverse district gave 60.3% to Kamala Harris and 63.1% to Colin Allred in 2024.

## District 8

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee Jessica Steinmann Laura Jones   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Morgan Luttrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morgan_Luttrell)  [Republican](32070.md) |

See also: [Texas's 8th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_8th_congressional_district)



Texas's 8th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The 8th district includes most of the northern and northwestern exurbs of [Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houston,_Texas), including [Conroe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conroe,_Texas), part of [Huntsville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huntsville,_Texas), [Willis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willis,_Texas), [Magnolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnolia,_Texas), [Brookshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brookshire,_Texas) and [Hempstead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hempstead,_Texas), along with parts of west Houston including Briar Forest, the western end of the [Energy Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houston_Energy_Corridor) and most of the Bear Creek and [Addicks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Addicks,_Houston) areas in west Harris County. The incumbent is Republican [Morgan Luttrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morgan_Luttrell), who was elected with 68.2% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] Luttrell is not seeking reelection in the heavily Republican district, which gave Donald Trump 63.2% and Ted Cruz 60.1% of the vote in 2024 and is a plurality White district with a 31.3% Hispanic voting age population.[^cite_note-\:1-105]

## District 9

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee Leticia Gutierrez Alex Mealer   Party [Democratic](5043544.md) [Republican](32070.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  *None* (New seat) |

See also: [Texas's 9th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_9th_congressional_district)



Texas's 9th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 9th district, which previously encompassed southern portions of [Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houston,_Texas) and such suburbs as [Missouri City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missouri_City,_Texas) and was represented by incumbent Democrat [Al Green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Green_\(politician\)), has been relocated to the eastern portions of the Houston area as a result of redistricting; Green ran for reelection in the newly redrawn 18th district.[^cite_note-HPVT-28]

The new district, which has a 58.6% Hispanic voting age population, covers such east and southeast Houston neighborhoods as [Denver Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denver_Harbor,_Houston), [Magnolia Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magnolia_Park,_Houston), [Park Place](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Park_Place,_Houston) and Edgebrook, as well as most of the suburbs of [Pasadena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pasadena,_Texas), [Baytown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baytown,_Texas), [Deer Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deer_Park,_Texas), [La Porte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Porte,_Texas), [Galena Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galena_Park,_Texas), [Channelview](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Channelview,_Texas) and [Crosby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crosby,_Texas), and exurban [Liberty County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_County,_Texas) including [Cleveland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cleveland,_Texas), [Liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty,_Texas) and [Dayton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dayton,_Texas). Donald Trump carried the district in all three of his elections - a 49.8% plurality in 2016, 53.7% in 2020, and 59.5% in 2024, and the district also gave Ted Cruz 54.4% of the vote in 2024.

## District 10

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee Chris Gober Caitlin Rourk   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Michael McCaul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_McCaul)  [Republican](32070.md) |

See also: [Texas's 10th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_10th_congressional_district)



Texas's 10th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 10th district stretches from downtown and western [Austin](1998.md) (including [Lake Travis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Travis)) through the [Bryan–College Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bryan%E2%80%93College_Station) area to a rural stretch of east-central Texas between Houston, Dallas and Tyler, including [Crockett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crockett,_Texas), [Livingston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Livingston,_Texas) and [Madisonville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Madisonville,_Texas). The district was redrawn during Texas's 2025 mid-decade congressional redistricting.[^cite_note-HPVT-28]

Chris Gober, who subsequently became the Republican nominee for the district, had participated professionally in Republican congressional redistricting efforts. During the 2021 Texas redistricting process, 22 Republican members of the [Texas Legislature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Legislature) retained Gober, who in turn retained the [National Republican Redistricting Trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Republican_Redistricting_Trust) and obtained the services of Republican mapmaker Adam Kincaid. According to Gober's [deposition](https://www.democracydocket.com/wp-content/uploads/2021/10/1303-2025-10-20-Transcript-of-day-6-of-9-afternoon-session.pdf), Kincaid operated the map-drawing software used for the congressional maps.[^cite_note-ProPublicaGober-162] He subsequently redrew the 10th Congressional district to include his own residence and those of his client, Elon Musk, as well as [the land](https://www.kbtx.com/2026/08/21/elon-musk-connected-company-now-controls-over-4600-acres-grimes-county/) Musk had already identified for his [Terafab Project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terafab).&#x20;

Through his co-founded nationwide law firm Lex Politica (formerly known as The Gober Group) Gober has represented numerous elected Republican officials in federal ethics investigations. Since 2024, he has served as the director and chief lawyer of entrepreneur Elon Musk's America PAC, including in support of then-nominee Donald Trump's presidential campaign. He defended the PAC against several lawsuits filed by state and city governments regarding its \$1 million lottery for voters in swing states. [^cite_note-163] Lex Politica also represented the non-profit arm in the US of [Victor Orban](750582.md)'s Danube Institute.[^cite_note-164]

Gober was also involved in the 2025 redistricting process. Republican mapmaker Kincaid testified that the [Republican National Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_National_Committee) retained Gober to represent the Texas Republican congressional delegation and that Gober conveyed the delegation's requests to him during the map-drawing process.[^cite_note-DemocracyDocketGober-165] Gober's congressional campaign subsequently listed his redistricting work among his accomplishments, stating that he had helped deliver a redistricting plan that strengthened the Republican majority and describing the result as having "Secured Five New Republican Seats."[^cite_note-GoberCampaignRedistricting-166]

Gober won the Republican nomination for Texas’ redrawn 10th Congressional District with 51.2 percent of the vote, narrowly avoiding a runoff.[^cite_note-167] Gober is running on a platform of strict border security, “America First” economic policies, and the “weaponization of government” against conservative causes[^cite_note-168]. The incumbent is Republican [Michael McCaul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_McCaul), who was re-elected in 2024 with 63.6% of the vote. McCaul is not seeking reelection in the redrawn district, which gave 60.5% of the vote to Donald Trump and 58.4% to Ted Cruz in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28]

Gober's campaign was predominantly self-financed. From October 14, 2025, through the March 3, 2026 primary, his campaign reported **\$2,265,179.06** in receipts, of which **\$1,524,821.91**, or approximately **67.3%**, came from Gober himself through candidate loans and contributions.[^cite_note-FECGoberReceipts-190][^cite_note-FECGoberCandidate-191][^cite_note-FECGoberLoans-192]

Of the remaining **\$740,357.15** in non-self-funded receipts, **\$374,365.91** was associated with addresses within the district, while **\$148,595.74** came from Texas addresses outside the district and **\$217,395.50** came from addresses outside Texas.[^cite_note-FECGoberIndividual-193][^cite_note-FECGoberPAC-194][^cite_note-FECGoberOtherPolitical-195][^cite_note-FECGoberTransfers-196][^cite_note-FECGoberOtherReceipts-197]

### Democratic primary

Caitlin Rourk, Democratic Nominee for the 10th Congressional District, grew up near Washington, D.C., as the daughter of two public school teachers. She served as an intelligence officer in the [Texas Army National Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Army_National_Guard) and [U.S. Army Reserve](1187028.md), participating in full-time missions supporting disaster response, border operations, and public health programs. Early in her career, Rourk engaged in bipartisan public policy work focused on deficit reduction and energy security, later leading corporate communications at [Dell Technologies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dell_Technologies). A resident of Central Texas for over a decade, she lives in the [Eanes Independent School District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eanes_Independent_School_District), where her children attend public school[^cite_note-198].

Rourk is the Democratic nominee running to represent Texas's 10th Congressional District in the 2026 election, winning her primary with more than [60% of the vote](https://ballotpedia.org/Caitlin_Rourk) for an open seat created by the retirement of long-serving Republican Representative [Michael McCaul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_McCaul). The newly drawn district encompasses 13 counties, stretching from the Houston suburbs through rural East Texas to northern Austin. Positioning herself as a [pragmatic problem solver](https://www.statesman.com/opinion/editorials/article/rourk-us-house-10-democrats-best-bet-pragmatic-21331845.php) and [servant leader](https://communityimpact.com/lake-travis-westlake/election/q-a-meet-the-candidates-for-us-house-district-10/), Rourk has centered her campaign on grassroots door-knocking, deep organizing, and restoring accountability through proposals such as mandating regular open town halls in every congressional district.

Rourk's legislative platform emphasizes economic stability for working families, advocating for strengthening the Child Tax Credit, expanding technical workforce training and apprenticeships, and indexing the federal minimum wage to the cost of living. On healthcare, she favors a public option, permanently expanding Affordable Care Act (ACA) subsidies, and allowing Medicare to negotiate prescription drug price. Her broader policy priorities include fully funding public schools while opposing private school voucher expansions, establishing a federal accountability framework for AI data center infrastructure, and passing comprehensive immigration reform that combines border enforcement with an earned path to citizenship for law-abiding residents[^cite_note-199].

## District 11

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [August Pfluger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_Pfluger) Claire Reynolds   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |

See also: [Texas's 11th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_11th_congressional_district)



Texas's 11th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 11th district is based in midwestern Texas, including [Midland](151222.md), [Odessa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odessa,_Texas), [San Angelo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Angelo) and [Brownwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brownwood,_Texas), and also includes a thin stretch of the Austin area along the Travis and Williamson county lines including [Pflugerville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pflugerville,_Texas) and [Horseshoe Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horseshoe_Bay,_Texas). The incumbent is Republican [August Pfluger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_Pfluger), who was re-elected unopposed in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] The majority White district has a voting age population that is 35.3% Hispanic, and in 2024 gave Donald Trump 66.5% of the vote and Ted Cruz 64%.

## District 12

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Craig Goldman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Craig_Goldman) Angela Rodriguez Prilliman   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |

See also: [Texas's 12th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_12th_congressional_district)



Texas's 12th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 12th district is in the western part of the [Dallas–Fort Worth metroplex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas%E2%80%93Fort_Worth_metroplex), and takes in most of [Parker County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parker_County,_Texas) and western [Tarrant County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarrant_County,_Texas), including most of the western half of [Fort Worth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Worth,_Texas) and such inner suburbs as [Benbrook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benbrook,_Texas), [Saginaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saginaw,_Texas), and [Haltom City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haltom_City,_Texas), as well as [Weatherford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weatherford,_Texas) in Parker County. The incumbent is Republican [Craig Goldman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Craig_Goldman), who was elected with 63.5% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] Donald Trump won 61.3% and Ted Cruz 57.9% in 2024.

The new 13th district encompasses most of the [Texas Panhandle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Panhandle) and the western part of the [Texoma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texoma) region, containing the cities of [Amarillo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amarillo,_Texas) and [Wichita Falls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wichita_Falls,_Texas), as well as the college town of [Denton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denton,_Texas) in Denton County. The incumbent is Republican [Ronny Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronny_Jackson), who was re-elected unopposed in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] Donald Trump won 72.5% of the vote and Ted Cruz 70.3% in this district.

## District 14

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Randy Weber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randy_Weber) Thurman Bartie   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |

See also: [Texas's 14th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_14th_congressional_district)



Texas's 14th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 14th district remains anchored in [Galveston County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galveston_County,_Texas) in the southeast corner of [Greater Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Houston), including [Galveston](53840.md), [League City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_City,_Texas), [Friendswood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friendswood,_Texas) and [Texas City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_City,_Texas), and also now extends westward to [Manvel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manvel,_Texas) and [Alvin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alvin,_Texas) in north central Brazoria County and southern [Missouri City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missouri_City,_Texas) in Fort Bend County, as well east across [Bolivar Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolivar_Peninsula,_Texas) to [Port Arthur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Arthur,_Texas) and [Orange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orange,_Texas) in the [Golden Triangle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beaumont%E2%80%93Port_Arthur_metropolitan_area) area. The incumbent is Republican [Randy Weber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randy_Weber), who was re-elected with 68.7% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] Donald Trump won 61.5 percent of the vote and Ted Cruz 58.6 in this district in 2024.

## District 15

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Monica De La Cruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monica_De_La_Cruz) [Bobby Pulido](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobby_Pulido)   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Monica De La Cruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monica_De_La_Cruz)  [Republican](32070.md) |

See also: [Texas's 15th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_15th_congressional_district)



Texas's 15th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 15th district stretches from [Hidalgo County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hidalgo_County,_Texas) in the [Rio Grande Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande_Valley_\(Texas\)) (including Edinburg and Weslaco), and now extends northeasterly into several rural counties between Corpus Christi, San Antonio and Victoria, including such communities as [Falfurrias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falfurrias,_Texas), [Alice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alice,_Texas), [Sinton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinton,_Texas), [Beeville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beeville,_Texas), [Cuero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuero,_Texas) and [Gonzales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gonzales,_Texas). The incumbent is Republican [Monica De La Cruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monica_De_La_Cruz), who was re-elected with 57.1% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28]

In 2024, Donald Trump won 58.5% in this overwhelmingly Hispanic district, which gave Ted Cruz 53.5% in the same election (six years after Cruz lost to Beto O'Rourke, who won 55.4% in the 2018 election for the same Senate seat). Hillary Clinton won the district with 55% in 2016 before it flipped to Trump (who won 50.7%) in 2020.

## District 16

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Veronica Escobar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veronica_Escobar) Adam Bauman   Party [Democratic](5043544.md) [Republican](32070.md) |

See also: [Texas's 16th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_16th_congressional_district)



Texas's 16th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 16th district is entirely within [El Paso County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Paso_County,_Texas), taking in [El Paso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Paso,_Texas) and such surrounding suburbs as [Socorro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socorro,_Texas), [Horizon City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horizon_City,_Texas), and [Anthony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthony,_Texas). The incumbent is Democrat [Veronica Escobar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veronica_Escobar), who was re-elected with 59.5% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] In 2024, Kamala Harris won 57.4% of the vote in this heavily Hispanic district, where Colin Allred also won with 58.4% of the vote.

## District 17

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Pete Sessions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pete_Sessions) Casey Shepard   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Pete Sessions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pete_Sessions)  [Republican](32070.md) |

See also: [Texas's 17th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_17th_congressional_district)



Texas's 17th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 17th district is anchored in [Waco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waco,_Texas) and its surrounding [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waco_metropolitan_area), with a small sliver of the district extending into the east side of Temple and a southern sliver of Williamson County, with the Austin suburb of [Cedar Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cedar_Park,_Texas) connected to the rest of the district via a small sliver of [Round Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Round_Rock,_Texas). The incumbent is Republican [Pete Sessions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pete_Sessions), who was re-elected with 66.4% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] The new district gave 60% of the vote to Donald Trump and 57.5% to Ted Cruz in 2024.

## District 18

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Christian Menefee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Menefee) Ronald Whitfield   Party [Democratic](5043544.md) [Republican](32070.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Al Green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Green_\(politician\)) [Christian Menefee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Menefee)  [Democratic](5043544.md) |

See also: [Texas's 18th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_18th_congressional_district)

See also: [2025–26 Texas's 18th congressional district special election](79387652.md)



Texas's 18th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 18th district has two incumbents: Democrat [Al Green](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Green_\(politician\)), who was re-elected unopposed in 2024 for the 9th district, and [Christian Menefee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Menefee) who succeeded [Sylvester Turner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sylvester_Turner), who died unexpectedly in March 2025, in a special election runoff held in [January 2026](79387652.md).[^cite_note-HPVT-28]

The new district, which has a voting age population that is 45% Black and 32.2% Hispanic, includes the [Downtown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Houston), [EaDo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Downtown_Houston), [Midtown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Midtown,_Houston), [Third Ward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Ward,_Houston) and [Fifth Ward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fifth_Ward,_Houston) portions of Houston as well as the [Texas Medical Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Medical_Center), the [Museum District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houston_Museum_District) and [NRG Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NRG_Stadium), and extends northeast to [Settegast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Settegast,_Houston) and [Fall Creek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fall_Creek,_Texas) in northeast Houston, [Sunnyside](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sunnyside,_Houston) and [Brays Oaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brays_Oaks,_Houston) in south and southwest Houston, and northern [Missouri City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Missouri_City,_Texas), [Stafford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stafford,_Texas) and [Fresno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fresno,_Texas) in Fort Bend County. In 2024, the district gave Kamala Harris 76.7% of the vote and 78.5% to Colin Allred.

## District 19

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee Tom Sell Kyle Rable   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Jodey Arrington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jodey_Arrington)  [Republican](32070.md) |

See also: [Texas's 19th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_19th_congressional_district)



Texas's 19th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 19th district, then as now, encompasses much of [West Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Texas) including [Lubbock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lubbock,_Texas) and [Abilene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abilene,_Texas) along with [Big Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Spring,_Texas). The incumbent is Republican [Jodey Arrington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jodey_Arrington), who was re-elected with 80.7% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] Arrington is not seeking reelection to a sixth term in the heavily Republican district, which gave 75.3% of the vote to Donald Trump and 73% to Ted Cruz in 2024, and is a majority White district with a voting age population that is 34.7% Hispanic.

## District 20

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Joaquin Castro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joaquin_Castro) Edgardo Baez   Party [Democratic](5043544.md) [Republican](32070.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Joaquin Castro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joaquin_Castro)  [Democratic](5043544.md) |

See also: [Texas's 20th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_20th_congressional_district)



Texas's 20th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 20th district encompasses downtown [San Antonio](53848.md) and extends eastward to its historically Black east side and the community of [Kirby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirby,_Texas), as well as westward to [Leon Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leon_Valley,_Texas) and several neighborhoods north of [Lackland AFB](405875.md). The incumbent is Democrat [Joaquin Castro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joaquin_Castro), who was re-elected unopposed in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] Kamala Harris won the two-thirds Hispanic district with 63.5% of the vote and Colin Allred 66.6% in 2024.

## District 21

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Mark Teixeira](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mark_Teixeira) Kristin Hook   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Chip Roy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chip_Roy)  [Republican](32070.md) |

See also: [Texas's 21st congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_21st_congressional_district)



Texas's 21st congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 21st district takes in the [Texas Hill Country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Hill_Country), including [Fredericksburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fredericksburg,_Texas), [Boerne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boerne,_Texas), [Kerrville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kerrville,_Texas) and [Bandera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bandera,_Texas), along with Comal County including [New Braunfels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Braunfels,_Texas) and most of Hays County including [San Marcos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Marcos,_Texas), [Wimberley](136045.md) and [Dripping Springs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dripping_Springs,_Texas), as well as most of northwest [San Antonio](53848.md) along with [Alamo Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alamo_Heights,_Texas), [Castle Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Castle_Hills,_Texas), the eastern half of [Stone Oak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stone_Oak,_San_Antonio) and [Fort Sam Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Sam_Houston) in Bexar County. The incumbent is Republican [Chip Roy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chip_Roy), who was elected with 61.9% of the vote in 2024 and [ran for Texas Attorney General in 2026](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Texas_Attorney_General_election), but lost in the primary.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] A Republican-held district since 1978, Donald Trump won 60.3% of the vote in this largely exurban district, which also gave Ted Cruz 57.7%, both in 2024.

## District 22

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee Trever Nehls Marquette Greene-Scott   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Troy Nehls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troy_Nehls)  [Republican](32070.md) |

See also: [Texas's 22nd congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_22nd_congressional_district)



Texas's 22nd congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 22nd district encompasses the southwest suburban corner of the [Greater Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Houston) metropolitan area across Harris, Fort Bend and Brazoria counties, including the southern [Houston](13774.md) suburbs of [Sugar Land](135900.md), [Rosenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rosenberg,_Texas), [Lake Jackson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Jackson,_Texas) and [Angleton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angleton,_Texas), as well as the [Katy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katy,_Texas) and [Fulshear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fulshear,_Texas) areas in both Harris and Fort Bend counties. The incumbent is Republican [Troy Nehls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Troy_Nehls), who is not seeking reelection and was re-elected with 62.1% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] That same year, Donald Trump won 59.9% and Ted Cruz 56.9% of the vote in the district, which is diverse with double-digit populations of White, Hispanic, Asian and Black residents (both voting age and overall).

## District 23

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Brandon Herrera](78312187.md) Katy Padilla Stout   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |
| **U.S. Representative before election**  [Tony Gonzales](65756758.md)  [Republican](32070.md) **Elected U.S. Representative**  TBD |

See also: [Texas's 23rd congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_23rd_congressional_district)



Texas's 23rd congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 23rd district covers southwestern Texas, including the [Big Bend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Bend_\(Texas\)), and stretches eastward through [Del Rio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Del_Rio,_Texas) and [Uvalde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uvalde,_Texas) to the northern [San Antonio](53848.md) suburbs including the west side of [Stone Oak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stone_Oak,_San_Antonio), [Shavano Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shavano_Park,_Texas) and [Camp Bullis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camp_Bullis) with a small finger extending eastward to [Lackland AFB](405875.md) in southwest San Antonio, and also westward to the eastern fringes of the [El Paso](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Paso,_Texas) suburbs. The incumbent is Republican [Tony Gonzales](65756758.md), who was re-elected with 62.3% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] Democrats are targeting the majority Hispanic district, which has a one-third White minority, and gave Donald Trump and Ted Cruz 56.8% and 52.9% of the vote, respectively, in 2024.

Republican leaders called on Gonzales to end his re-election campaign after he admitted to having a sexual relationship with a former staff member who later died by suicide, following his earlier denial of the allegations.[^cite_note-352][^cite_note-353] Gonzales ended his campaign on March 5,[^cite_note-354] leading to the cancellation of the runoff and making challenger Brandon Herrera the Republican nominee.&#x20;

Gonzales later resigned on April 13 after he was expected to face an expulsion vote alongside Democratic representative [Eric Swalwell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_Swalwell), who was accused of sexual assault.[^cite_note-355] Swalwell similarly had announced his resignation on April 13, hours before Gonzales' announcement.[^cite_note-356][^cite_note-357] Despite multiple other vacancies which occurred around the same time having been filled by August, governor Greg Abbott never called an emergency special election to fill this seat, likely leaving it vacant until January 2027.[^cite_note-358]

## District 24

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Beth Van Duyne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beth_Van_Duyne) Kevin Burge   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Beth Van Duyne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beth_Van_Duyne) ([Republican](32070.md))  [Julie Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julie_Johnson_\(politician\)) ([Democratic](5043544.md)) |

See also: [Texas's 24th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_24th_congressional_district)



Texas's 24th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 24th district, centered on [Dallas Fort Worth International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas_Fort_Worth_International_Airport) in the heart of the [Dallas–Fort Worth metroplex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas%E2%80%93Fort_Worth_metroplex), encompasses the suburbs north of [Fort Worth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Worth) and [Dallas](53838.md), including [Grapevine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grapevine,_Texas), [Bedford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bedford,_Texas), [North Richland Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Richland_Hills,_Texas) and [Southlake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southlake,_Texas) in northeast Tarrant County, and the wealthy [Park Cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Park_Cities,_Texas) north of downtown Dallas, as well as the neighboring [Knox Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knox_Park,_Dallas) and [Lower Greenville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_Greenville,_Dallas) neighborhoods and most of [north Dallas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Dallas) (including [Preston Hollow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preston_Hollow,_Dallas)) in Dallas itself and the Dallas County suburbs of [Farmers Branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farmers_Branch,_Texas) and [Coppell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coppell,_Texas).

Due to redistricting, the district has two incumbents, Republican [Beth Van Duyne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beth_Van_Duyne), who was re-elected with 60.3% of the vote in 2024, and Democrat [Julie Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julie_Johnson_\(politician\)), who was elected with 61.9% of the vote in 2024 from the old 32nd District.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] Donald Trump won 57.1% of the vote in this affluent district, which also gave Ted Cruz 54.6% of the vote that same year against Democrat [Colin Allred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colin_Allred), whom Johnson succeeded in Congress. Johnson has since decided to seek reelection in the newly redrawn 33rd district (see below).

## District 25

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Roger Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Williams_\(Texas_politician\)) Dione Sims   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Marc Veasey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc_Veasey) ([Democratic](5043544.md)) [Roger Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Williams_\(Texas_politician\)) ([Republican](32070.md)) |

See also: [Texas's 25th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_25th_congressional_district)



Texas's 25th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 25th district runs from northern [Arlington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arlington,_Texas) and southern and eastern [Fort Worth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Worth,_Texas) in Tarrant County, whose portion is the only portion of the district considered even remotely competitive (and in fact, favorable) to Democrats, out to several heavily Republican exurban and rural areas south and west of Fort Worth and just east of Abilene, including [Cleburne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cleburne,_Texas), [Granbury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Granbury,_Texas), [Willow Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willow_Park,_Texas), [Mineral Wells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mineral_Wells,_Texas), [Stephenville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephenville,_Texas), [Jacksboro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacksboro,_Texas) and [Eastland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastland,_Texas).

Due to redistricting, the district has two incumbents, Republican [Roger Williams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Williams_\(Texas_politician\)), who was re-elected unopposed in 2024, and Democrat [Marc Veasey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc_Veasey), who was re-elected with 68.7% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] Veasey, the incumbent from the old 33rd district (see below) decided to not seek reelection, instead pursuing a short-lived bid for Tarrant County judge before dropping out of that race. Donald Trump won 61.4% of the vote in this district in 2024, which also saw Ted Cruz win 58.4% of the vote.

Texas's 26th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 26th district is based in the northwestern corner of the [Dallas–Fort Worth metroplex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas%E2%80%93Fort_Worth_metroplex), centering on southern and eastern [Denton County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denton_County,_Texas) (including the county's share of [Carrollton](151136.md) along with all of [Lewisville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lewisville,_Texas), [Flower Mound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flower_Mound,_Texas) and [Little Elm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Elm,_Texas)) and including [Cooke County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooke_County,_Texas) ([Gainesville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gainesville,_Texas)) and the southern two-thirds of [Wise County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wise_County,_Texas) including [Decatur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decatur,_Texas). The incumbent is Republican [Brandon Gill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandon_Gill), who was elected with 62.1% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] That same year, the district gave 61.2% of the vote to Donald Trump and 58.4% to Ted Cruz.

## District 27

|  |

|  |
| Nominee [Michael Cloud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Cloud) Tanya Lloyd   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |

See also: [Texas's 27th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_27th_congressional_district)



Texas's 27th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 27th district stretches across the [Coastal Bend](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Coastal_Bend), from downtown [Corpus Christi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corpus_Christi,_Texas) and [Port Aransas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_Aransas,_Texas) in the south, along with [Victoria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria,_Texas) and the rural fringes of the Greater Houston area including [Brenham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brenham,_Texas), [Bay City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bay_City,_Texas) and [Sealy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sealy,_Texas), extending westward to [La Grange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Grange,_Texas) along with the southern and eastern suburbs of Austin including [Bastrop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bastrop,_Texas), [Kyle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyle,_Texas) and [Lockhart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockhart,_Texas) along with an eastern sliver of Travis County (including the [Circuit of the Americas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circuit_of_the_Americas)).

The incumbent is Republican [Michael Cloud](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Cloud), who was re-elected with 66.0% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] A plurality White district whose voting age population is more than 40% Hispanic, Donald Trump won 60% of the vote and Ted Cruz 57.1% in 2024.

## District 28

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Henry Cuellar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Cuellar) Tano Tijerina   Party [Democratic](5043544.md) [Republican](32070.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Henry Cuellar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Cuellar)  [Democratic](5043544.md) |

See also: [Texas's 28th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_28th_congressional_district)



Texas's 28th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 28th district is based in the [Laredo](136773.md) area and stretches south to [McAllen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McAllen,_Texas) in the [Rio Grande Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande_Valley_\(Texas\)) and north to [Atascosa County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atascosa_County,_Texas) in the San Antonio area. The incumbent is Democrat [Henry Cuellar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Cuellar), who was re-elected with 52.8% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28]

Historically a heavily Democratic district, Donald Trump won 54.8% of the vote in this overwhelmingly Hispanic district in 2024, which also saw Ted Cruz win a plurality of 48.8% (and a vote margin of only 228 votes) that same year; the district previously gave Democrats Joe Biden 54.3% in 2020 and Hillary Clinton 66.4% in 2016. In U.S. Senate races, John Cornyn lost the district twice in 2014 and 2020 for his seat, as did Cruz in his seat in 2018 against Beto O'Rourke, who won 65.8% of the vote that year.

Texas's 29th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 29th district encompasses much of north [Houston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houston,_Texas), taking in the heavily Latino [Lindale Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lindale_Park,_Houston) and [Northline](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northline,_Houston) areas along with historically Black [Acres Homes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acres_Homes,_Houston) and [Independence Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Heights,_Houston), as well as the [Garden Oaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garden_Oaks,_Houston), [Oak Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oak_Forest,_Houston) and [Fairbanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairbanks,_Houston) areas of northwest Houston, and the [Aldine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aldine,_Texas) and [Greenspoint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenspoint,_Houston) areas of far north Houston including [George Bush Intercontinental Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Bush_Intercontinental_Airport). The incumbent is Democrat [Sylvia Garcia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sylvia_Garcia), who was re-elected with 65.2% of the vote in 2024 in the majority Hispanic district, which was won by Kamala Harris (64.5%) and Colin Allred (67.6%) that same year.[^cite_note-HPVT-28]

#### Nominee

- [Sylvia Garcia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sylvia_Garcia), incumbent U.S. representative

  [^cite_note-Sylvia-439]

- [Jarvis Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jarvis_Johnson), former [state representative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_House_of_Representatives) from the [139th district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_139th_House_of_Representatives_district) (2016–2025), candidate for this district in [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_United_States_House_of_Representatives_elections_in_Texas#District_18), candidate for [Texas's 15th Senate district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Senate,_District_15) in the 2024 [special](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_state_legislative_elections#Texas_2) and [regular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Texas_Senate_election) elections, and candidate for the 18th district in [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_House_of_Representatives_elections_in_Texas#Replacement_nominee_selection)

  [^cite_note-Sylvia-439]

- Robert Slater, business owner and candidate for the 18th district in [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_United_States_House_of_Representatives_elections_in_Texas#District_18) and [2025](79387652.md)

## District 30

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Frederick Haynes III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frederick_Haynes_III) Everett Jackson   Party [Democratic](5043544.md) [Republican](32070.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  *None* (New seat) |

See also: [Texas's 30th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_30th_congressional_district)



Texas's 30th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 30th district is anchored in the southern portions of [Dallas](53838.md) and encompasses [South Dallas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Dallas) and [Fair Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fair_Park), stretching southward to such diverse south Dallas County suburbs as [Lancaster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lancaster,_Texas), [Duncanville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duncanville,_Texas), [DeSoto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DeSoto,_Texas), [Wilmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilmer,_Texas), [Cedar Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cedar_Hill,_Texas) and southern [Grand Prairie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Prairie,_Texas). Prior to redistricting, the incumbent was Democrat [Jasmine Crockett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jasmine_Crockett); however, Crockett was drawn out of the 30th district and into the 33rd and ultimately decided to run for the U.S. Senate. Kamala Harris won 72.7% of the vote in the district in 2024, as did Dallas native Colin Allred who won 75.1% in his losing Senate bid against Ted Cruz.

See also: [Texas's 31st congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_31st_congressional_district)



Texas's 31st congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 31st district is anchored in the northern exurbs of [Austin](1998.md) (including [Georgetown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgetown,_Texas) and [Burnet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burnet,_Texas)) and stretches northward to [Killeen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killeen,_Texas) and most of [Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple,_Texas) along with [Fort Hood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Hood), going as far north as [Hamilton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamilton,_Texas). The incumbent is Republican [John Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Carter_\(Texas_politician\)), who was re-elected with 64.5% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] That same year, Donald Trump won 60.1% and Ted Cruz 57.6% of the vote, respectively.

## District 32

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee Dan Barrios Jace Yarbrough   Party [Democratic](5043544.md) [Republican](32070.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  *None* (New seat) |

See also: [Texas's 32nd congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_32nd_congressional_district)



Texas's 32nd congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 32nd district covers several suburban areas directly north and northeast of [Dallas](53838.md), including most of the [Dallas County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas_County,_Texas) portion of [Carrollton](151136.md) and [Addison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Addison,_Texas) along with all of [Richardson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richardson,_Texas) (including the [Collin County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collin_County,_Texas) portion) and the northern halves of [Garland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garland,_Texas) and [Rowlett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rowlett,_Texas), as well as the [far north side](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Far_North_Dallas) of [Dallas](53838.md) itself. The district then stretches eastward and crosses [Lake Ray Hubbard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Ray_Hubbard) to take in suburban [Rockwall County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockwall_County,_Texas) and the [Lake Tawakoni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake_Tawakoni) area, traveling all the way east to such northern Tyler exurbs as [Mineola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mineola,_Texas) and [Gilmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilmer,_Texas).

Prior to redistricting, the incumbent was Democrat [Julie Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julie_Johnson_\(politician\)); however, Johnson was drawn out of the 32nd district and into the 24th, ultimately deciding to seek reelection in the 33rd district (see below) and leaving this district as an open seat.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] In 2024, the new district gave 57.7% of the vote to Donald Trump and 55.2% to Ted Cruz, with Democrats only considered competitive in the Dallas County and Richardson portion of the otherwise heavily Republican district.

## District 33

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Colin Allred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colin_Allred) Patrick Gillespie   Party [Democratic](5043544.md) [Republican](32070.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Jasmine Crockett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jasmine_Crockett)  [Democratic](5043544.md) |

See also: [Texas's 33rd congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_33rd_congressional_district)



Texas's 33rd congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 33rd district, previously encompassing mostly Hispanic parts of the [Dallas–Fort Worth metroplex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas%E2%80%93Fort_Worth_metroplex) including [Downtown Fort Worth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Fort_Worth), western [Dallas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas,_Texas), and parts of [Grand Prairie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Prairie,_Texas), [Irving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irving,_Texas), [Carrollton](151136.md), and [Farmers Branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farmers_Branch,_Texas), has since been redrawn to be exclusively within Dallas County. Prior to redistricting, the incumbent was Fort Worth-based Democrat [Marc Veasey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc_Veasey). However, Veasey was drawn out of the 33rd district and into the 25th, and ultimately chose to pursue a short-lived bid for Tarrant County judge before abandoning that bid. The new incumbent is Democrat [Jasmine Crockett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jasmine_Crockett), who was elected with 84.9% of the vote in 2024, in her previous District 30; however, Crockett chose to pursue a run for the United States Senate seat currently held by John Cornyn.[^cite_note-HPVT-28]

Former congressman and 2024 U.S. Senate nominee [Colin Allred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colin_Allred) (who lost in the general election that year to [Ted Cruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ted_Cruz)) and current 32nd District incumbent [Julie Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Julie_Johnson_\(politician\)) ran for the Democratic nomination to represent this district, which is centered in [Downtown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Dallas) and [Uptown Dallas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uptown,_Dallas) and also extends in four separate directions - northwest to [Love Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dallas_Love_Field) and a northern section of [Irving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Irving,_Texas) centered on the Valley Ranch area, northeast to the [Swiss Avenue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Swiss_Avenue_Historic_District) and [Buckner Boulevard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_State_Highway_Loop_12) (east of [White Rock Lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_Rock_Lake)) corridors in [east Dallas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Dallas), southeast to southeast Dallas (including [Pleasant Grove](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pleasant_Grove,_Dallas)) and [Balch Springs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balch_Springs,_Texas), and southwest to [West Dallas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Dallas) as well as [Cockrell Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cockrell_Hill,_Texas) and central [Grand Prairie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Prairie,_Texas). The new district gave 65.2% of the vote to Kamala Harris and 68.7% to Allred in 2024, and is over 50 percent Hispanic. Allred defeated Johnson in the runoff with 55% of the vote.

## District 34

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Vicente Gonzalez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vicente_Gonzalez_\(American_politician\)) Eric Flores   Party [Democratic](5043544.md) [Republican](32070.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Vicente Gonzalez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vicente_Gonzalez_\(American_politician\))  [Democratic](5043544.md) |

See also: [Texas's 34th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_34th_congressional_district)



Texas's 34th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 34th district stretches from [Brownsville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brownsville,_Texas) in the [Rio Grande Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rio_Grande_Valley_\(Texas\)), northward along the [Gulf Coast](11969.md) to most of [Corpus Christi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corpus_Christi,_Texas), covering all of [Cameron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cameron_County,_Texas), [Willacy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willacy_County,_Texas), [Kenedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenedy_County,_Texas), [Kleberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kleberg_County,_Texas) and most of [Nueces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nueces_County,_Texas) counties, essentially a recreation of the old 27th district from its establishment in 1982 until the 2010 election. The incumbent is Democrat [Vicente Gonzalez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vicente_Gonzalez_\(American_politician\)), who was re-elected with 51.3% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28]

Donald Trump won the district with 54.6% of the vote in 2024, having previously lost the district to Hillary Clinton and Joe Biden in 2016 and 2020, respectively, with Barack Obama winning the district twice in 2008 and 2012. Also, Ted Cruz won the district with a 49.7% plurality in 2024, even though the district gave 55% of the vote to Beto O'Rourke over Cruz in 2018.

## District 35

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee Johnny Garcia Carlos De La Cruz   Party [Democratic](5043544.md) [Republican](32070.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  *None* (New seat) |

See also: [Texas's 35th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_35th_congressional_district)



Texas's 35th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 35th district previously connected eastern [San Antonio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Antonio,_Texas) to southeastern [Austin](1998.md), through the [I-35](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I-35) corridor. However, with the new redistricting the 35th has been moved significantly to the south and east; it now covers much of south and northeast San Antonio, plus such suburbs as [Live Oak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Live_Oak,_Texas), [Converse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Converse,_Texas) and [Elmendorf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elmendorf,_Texas) along with [Guadalupe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guadalupe_County,_Texas) (including [Seguin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seguin,_Texas) and [Schertz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Schertz,_Texas)), [Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilson_County,_Texas) and [Karnes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karnes_County,_Texas) counties.

Prior to redistricting, the incumbent was Democrat [Greg Casar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greg_Casar); however, Casar was drawn out of the 35th district and into the 37th.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] The new district remains majority Hispanic, albeit with over 53.7% of the voting age population being Hispanic, and 34.6% of the voting age population being White. [Donald Trump](4848272.md) won 54.6% of the vote in this district in 2024, having won by single-digit margins here in both 2016 and 2020, while [Ted Cruz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ted_Cruz) won 50.6% of the vote in this district in 2024.

#### Campaign

The Democratic runoff race for this district gained national attention in May 2026 after multiple comments made by candidate Maureen Galindo, who led the first round of the primary, were condemned by state and national Democrats for antisemitism. On social media, Galindo pledged to turn an [ICE Detention Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_immigrant_detention_sites_in_the_United_States) into a "prison for American Zionists", and later suggested on a radio broadcast that primary rival Johnny Garcia should be tried for [treason](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treason_laws_in_the_United_States) due to his alleged support from Israel.[^cite_note-574][^cite_note-Kamisar-575]

Following the remarks, John Lira, a former primary opponent who had previously endorsed Galindo, rescinded his endorsement.[^cite_note-JTA150526-576] Prominent Democrats directly rebuked Galindo's candidacy or endorsed Garcia, including U.S. Representatives [Alexandria Ocasio-Cortez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandria_Ocasio-Cortez), [Suzan DelBene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suzan_DelBene) and [Hakeem Jeffries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakeem_Jeffries), alongside Texas state representatives [James Talarico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Talarico) and [Gina Hinojosa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gina_Hinojosa), who are the Democratic nominees in the concurrent [Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_United_States_Senate_election_in_Texas) and the [gubernatorial elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026_Texas_gubernatorial_election) respectively.[^cite_note-Kamisar-575][^cite_note-JTA150526-576][^cite_note-MGACAD-577]

## District 36

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Brian Babin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Babin) Rhonda Hart   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Brian Babin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Babin)  [Republican](32070.md) |

See also: [Texas's 36th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_36th_congressional_district)



Texas's 36th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 36th district encompasses parts of [Southeast Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_Texas), including the Harris County side of the [Clear Lake region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clear_Lake_\(region\)) of Houston. While largely unchanged from its previous iteration, the new iteration includes [Lufkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lufkin,_Texas) and the surrounding Piney Woods region as well as [Silsbee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silsbee,_Texas), [Jasper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jasper,_Texas) and most of [Beaumont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beaumont,_Texas), and also extends to almost all of [Chambers County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chambers_County,_Texas) (including [Mont Belvieu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mont_Belvieu,_Texas)) east of Houston) before extending into southeast [Houston](13774.md) (including [Hobby Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_P._Hobby_Airport) and [Ellington Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ellington_Field_Joint_Reserve_Base), as well as [Glenbrook Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glenbrook_Valley,_Houston) and the aforementioned [Clear Lake City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clear_Lake_City_\(Greater_Houston\)) development) along with the southeast [Harris County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harris_County,_Texas) communities of [Seabrook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seabrook,_Texas), [Webster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Webster,_Texas) and Harris County's portion of [Friendswood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friendswood,_Texas), and a small sliver of northern Brazoria County centered on most of [Pearland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pearland,_Texas).

The incumbent is Republican [Brian Babin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brian_Babin), who was re-elected with 69.4% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] Donald Trump won 61.8% of the vote in the new district in 2024, as did Ted Cruz with 59.1% of the vote.

## District 37

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee [Greg Casar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greg_Casar) Lauren Peña   Party [Democratic](5043544.md) [Republican](32070.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Greg Casar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greg_Casar) ([Democratic](5043544.md)) [Lloyd Doggett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Doggett) ([Democratic](5043544.md)) |

See also: [Texas's 37th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_37th_congressional_district)



Texas's 37th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 37th district is based in all but the westernmost parts of [Austin](1998.md), with virtually all of the precincts of the exclusively [Travis County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Travis_County,_Texas)-based district favoring Democrats to varying degrees; the new district has a White plurality with a 34% Hispanic voting age population. The incumbent is Democrat [Lloyd Doggett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Doggett), who was re-elected with 75.9% of the vote in 2024.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] On August 21, 2025, Doggett announced that he would not seek re-election due to mid-decade redistricting, and fellow Democratic Rep. [Greg Casar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greg_Casar) being moved into the 37th district.[^cite_note-auto-614] On August 25, 2025, Casar announced his bid for re-election from this district.[^cite_note-CasarHill-571] Kamala Harris won 76.8% of the vote in the new 37th District, which also gave 79.2% of the vote to Colin Allred; in both cases, the highest of any district amongst the state's new congressional districts.

## District 38

|  |
| --- |
|  |
| Nominee Jon Bonck Melissa McDonough   Party [Republican](32070.md) [Democratic](5043544.md) |
| **Incumbent U.S. Representative**  [Wesley Hunt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wesley_Hunt)  [Republican](32070.md) |

See also: [Texas's 38th congressional district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas's_38th_congressional_district)



Texas's 38th congressional district boundary from the 2026 elections\
[Interactive map version](78639082.md)

The new 38th district, much like its original iteration first used in the 2022 election (and historically the base of the original Houston-based iteration of the 7th district from 1966 until the 2022 redistricting), is based in west [Houston](13774.md) and northwest [Harris County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harris_County,_Texas), including all or parts of the west Houston neighborhoods of [River Oaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/River_Oaks,_Houston), [Tanglewood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanglewood,_Houston), [Memorial City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorial_City,_Houston), [Spring Branch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spring_Branch,_Houston) and the [Energy Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Houston_Energy_Corridor), as well as the communities of [Jersey Village](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey_Village,_Texas), [Copperfield](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copperfield,_Texas), [Cypress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cypress,_Texas), [Champion Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champion_Forest,_Texas), [Klein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klein,_Texas) and [Tomball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomball,_Texas) in northwest Harris County.

The incumbent is Republican [Wesley Hunt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wesley_Hunt), who was re-elected with 62.9% of the vote in 2024 and ran for the Republican nomination for U.S. Senate in 2026.[^cite_note-HPVT-28] Donald Trump won the district in 2024 with 59.5% of the vote, as did Ted Cruz (who resides in the district) with 56.6% of the vote.
